    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42, F84.0, F20.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... I guess they brought me in because of what's been happening at the warehouse. I keep doing these things, you know? Like, I'll just freeze with my arms up right by the forklift. And the forklifts, they're yellow, mostly. Though I saw a blue one once when I was helping my buddy move out of his apartment. He had this crazy cat that kept jumping on the boxes. Anyway, I've been copying the beeping sounds the forklifts make, and the hand signals, too. Or I'll start moving the boxes in these strange little patterns. Even when everyone is yelling for me to stop, I just... I keep doing it. It's like the boxes have to go a certain way. Reminds me of how my mom used to arrange her ceramic frogs on the windowsill. She had about fifty of them, all staring at the driveway...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things, like moving the boxes in certain patterns or copying the beeping sounds?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't really know if I *have* to. I haven't really noticed feeling like that, you know? I'm not really sure. It just sort of happens. But speaking of having to do things, the supervisor at the warehouse is always on us about the safety rules. Like, you have to wear these specific steel-toed boots. \n\nI got mine at this surplus store out on Highway 4. The guy who runs it, he keeps a live parrot right next to the cash register. Just sitting there on a little wooden stand. It was eating sunflower seeds, which made a huge mess all over the floor. I had a parakeet when I was little, but it escaped out the screen door because my brother left it open when he was bringing in the groceries. He dropped a whole gallon of milk on the porch that day. The plastic jug just exploded everywhere. It took forever to wash it off the wood, and it smelled sour for like a month...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or bothered by these things you're doing at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: Upset? No, I don't... I don't think it bothers me. I'm not really upset about it, it's just what happens, you know? But you know what actually does bother me? The vending machine in the break room. It always eats my dollar bills. I tried to get a ginger ale on Tuesday, and the machine just swallowed my money and started making this loud humming noise. \n\nIt sounded exactly like the lawnmower my dad used to have. It was this old red Honda mower, and you had to pull the cord like ten times just to get the engine to catch. The grass in our backyard was always way too tall because we'd put off mowing it until the city threatened to fine us. My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, he'd always glare at us over the chain-link fence when we finally did it. He grew these massive heirloom tomatoes on the other side. One time a squirrel took a single bite out of his biggest tomato and left it sitting right on the fence post...\n\nDoctor: Do you think these things you're doing at the warehouse are causing problems for you in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose it is causing some problems. Like, I’ve lost my forklift duties at the warehouse. People keep telling me they can’t count on me or keep things safe around me anymore. It’s a real pain. And I’ve fallen way behind in my community-college classes, too. I’m taking this astronomy class, and the professor always wears these bright yellow bowties. It looks exactly like the color of a school bus. I used to ride bus number 42 when I was in middle school, and the driver, Mrs. Gable, she always chewed that really loud bubblegum. The pink kind that smells like medicine? \n\nAnyway, I also stopped showing up for my shifts at the church food-bank because of the whole... people saying they can't count on me thing. Which is weird because all we do at the food bank is stack cans of soup. Mostly chicken noodle. I had chicken noodle soup when I got the flu last year, and I had to stay in bed for four days watching old game shows. The Price is Right is the best one, especially when they play the Plinko game. I always wondered how heavy those little chips actually are, you know? They bounce around so much before they hit the bottom...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to achieve something specific or follow a certain rule when you're moving the boxes in those strange patterns?\n\nPatient: A rule? Um... no, I don't think so. I'm not really trying to achieve anything, and there's no rulebook I'm following or anything like that. It's just... I just do it. But speaking of rulebooks, my roommate bought this massive board game last weekend, and the manual for it was like forty pages long. It was ridiculous. \n\nWe tried playing it on the living room floor, but the carpet in our apartment is really shaggy. It's that weird, bright orange shag carpet from the seventies? My landlord absolutely refuses to replace it. He's this grumpy guy who drives a huge white pickup truck, and he always takes up two parking spaces right in front of the building. One time he left a half-eaten sandwich on the dashboard, and it just baked in the sun for like three days straight. It was turkey and swiss, I'm pretty sure. I can't stand swiss cheese, honestly. It has all those holes in it, which just seems like you're getting ripped off, you know? Like you're paying for air. I buy the block cheddar from the deli down the street instead. The lady who works there has this giant tattoo of a compass on her forearm...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these things you're doing are causing you a lot of stress or anxiety in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Stress? Anxiety? Um, no, I don't... I don't really feel stressed about the boxes. It's not like my heart is racing or anything. It's just what I do. But you know what actually does stress me out? The bus schedule in this city. It's completely unpredictable. I was waiting at the stop on 4th Street yesterday, and the sign said the bus would be there in ten minutes, but I ended up standing there for almost an hour. \n\nIt started drizzling, too, and I didn't have an umbrella. I used to have this really good umbrella, it was golf-sized and had these green and white stripes. I left it on a park bench last summer when I was feeding the ducks. There's this one duck at the pond, he has a weird little tuft of feathers on his head that makes him look like he's wearing a tiny hat. People always throw bread at him, but you're really supposed to give them peas or corn. Bread is actually terrible for their stomachs. I read that in a magazine at the dentist's office once. It was an old issue of National Geographic, the one with the penguins on the cover. Penguins are fascinating, the way they huddle together for warmth...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming urge to repeat certain actions or behaviors outside of the warehouse, like the way you arrange things or copy sounds?\n\nPatient: Urges? Like, outside of work? Um... no, not really. I don't feel like I have to do those things when I'm not at the warehouse. It's not like an urge or anything. But speaking of urges, I had this massive craving for a chili dog the other night. Like, out of nowhere. \n\nI ended up walking all the way down to that late-night diner on 5th Avenue, the one with the flickering neon sign. The 'E' in 'Diner' is completely burned out, so it just says 'Din r.' It's been like that for at least two years. Anyway, the guy working the grill there, he's got this huge bushy beard, and he always wears a baseball cap backwards. It’s a Dodgers cap, which is weird because we’re thousands of miles away from Los Angeles. I’ve never been to California, but I’ve heard the traffic there is an absolute nightmare. My cousin Greg moved out there to try and become a screenwriter, but he ended up just working at a car wash. He said he washed a celebrity's car once, but he couldn't remember who it was. It was a silver Porsche, though. Porsches are nice, but they seem really impractical if you need to buy groceries or move furniture...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or unhappy when you're not able to move the boxes in those specific patterns?\n\nPatient: Upset? Um, no. If I can't move them like that, I just... I don't. It doesn't make me unhappy or anything. But you know what actually did make me really upset? The vending machine in the break room at the warehouse. It totally ate my dollar on Tuesday. I just wanted a bag of those spicy jalapeño chips, but the metal coil stopped turning right when the bag was at the very edge. It just hung there. \n\nI tried shaking the machine, but it's like, bolted to the wall or something. My buddy Dave tried to help me reach up inside the flap to grab it, but his arm got stuck for a second. Dave's a pretty big guy, he used to play defensive tackle in high school. He still wears his varsity jacket everywhere, even though the leather on the sleeves is all cracked and it's missing a button. Buttons are so annoying when they fall off, I never know how to sew them back on. My grandma tried to teach me once, she had this old blue metal tin that used to have Danish butter cookies in it, but it was just full of needles and thread. It's such a massive disappointment when you open one of those tins expecting a cookie and it's just a bunch of sewing stuff...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or unhappy when you're not able to copy the beeping sounds or hand signals from the forklifts?\n\nPatient: Upset about the beeping? No, not really. If I can't make the noises or do the signals, it's fine. I don't get mad about it or anything. But speaking of beeping, the microwave in my apartment is driving me completely insane. It has this super high-pitched beep when the timer finishes, and it just keeps going every minute until you open the door. \n\nI was just trying to heat up a Hot Pocket a few days ago—the pepperoni kind, which is obviously the best, even though the crust always gets rock hard on the ends. I got a giant box of them at that wholesale club out on Route 9. You have to buy literally everything in bulk there. I saw this guy with a flatbed cart buying like, fifty giant bottles of ketchup. I have no idea who needs that much ketchup unless they run a hot dog stand or something. My uncle actually used to own a hot dog stand near the boardwalk, but he had to sell it because the city raised the permit fees. The city council is always raising taxes on stuff. They spent all this money on a new modern art statue for the park, but it just looks like a giant rusty metal pretzel. Pigeons sit on it all day, and pigeons are actually really smart birds, they can recognize human faces. I saw a video about it when I was taking a biology class at the community college. We had to use these really old microscopes that barely focused...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed when you cannot copy the beeping sounds or move the boxes in the specific patterns you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Distressed? No, I don't... I don't feel distressed. If I can't do the patterns or the sounds, it's just whatever. It doesn't bother me. But man, talking about distress, you know what really stresses me out? Trying to find a parking spot near my apartment building after 6 PM. It's impossible. \n\nThe street sweepers come on Thursdays, so everyone has to move their cars to the other side of the street, and it turns into this huge game of musical chairs. Last week, I had to park like four blocks away near this old laundromat. The laundromat has these massive industrial dryers that smell like burnt lint and cheap fabric softener. I used to go there before I got my own washer, but this lady would always hog like three folding tables at once. She had these tiny little yappy dogs she’d bring in, even though there was a huge 'No Pets' sign on the door. People just ignore signs all the time. Like at the warehouse, there's a sign that says 'Maximum Capacity 15 People' in the break room, but I swear there were like twenty of us in there during the holiday rush. The holidays are always so crazy with shipping, everyone ordering last-minute gifts online. I ordered my mom a scarf last year, but it got lost in the mail for three weeks. The tracking info just said 'In Transit' forever. I hate when it just says 'In Transit' and doesn't tell you where it actually is...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must perform certain rituals or behaviors in a specific way, even when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Like... doing things a certain way outside of work? Um, no, not really. I don't have anything like that when I'm just at home or out and about. But speaking of doing things a certain way, my neighbor across the hall is completely obsessed with his front door mat. He has to have it lined up perfectly with the edges of the doorframe. \n\nI saw him out there the other day with a tape measure, I'm not even kidding. A yellow Stanley tape measure. My dad used to have one of those, he kept it in this massive red toolbox in the garage. That toolbox weighed a ton because he never threw away any rusty screws or bent nails. He always said, 'You never know when you'll need a good nail.' But they weren't good, they were all crooked! We tried to build a birdhouse once when I was like, ten. We used those crooked nails and the roof ended up completely lopsided. A squirrel ended up moving into it instead of a bird. Squirrels are actually crazy agile, I watched one jump from a telephone wire to a tree branch that was like ten feet away. It just launched itself into the air like a tiny acrobat. I went to a circus once with acrobats, but the tent smelled super weird, kind of like old popcorn and damp hay...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you must perform certain actions or behaviors, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Actions I don't want to do? No, I don't think so. I just do what I need to do, you know? I don't feel forced to do anything weird. But talking about doing things you don't want to do, I had to help my buddy Dave move last weekend, and let me tell you, I really did not want to do that. He lives on the fourth floor of this walk-up building, and the stairs are super narrow. \n\nWe had to pivot this giant, ugly plaid couch around like three different corners. It was like that scene in Friends, you know, where they're yelling 'Pivot! Pivot!' I always thought that show was kind of overrated, to be honest. Like, how did they afford those huge apartments in New York City? New York is so expensive. I went there once for a school trip in middle school, and I bought a hot dog from a street cart that cost like eight dollars. Eight dollars! And the bun was kind of soggy. It had that weird yellow mustard on it, the kind that stains your shirt forever if you drop it. I ruined my favorite band t-shirt that way. It was a Foo Fighters shirt, the one with the alien on it. Aliens are a whole other thing, man. Have you seen those declassified Navy videos? The ones with the little tic-tac shaped things flying around? They move way too fast to be our jets. My cousin thinks they're from underwater bases, not space. He spends all day on these weird internet forums reading about deep-sea trenches. The ocean is crazy deep, like, there are fish down there that glow in the dark because sunlight doesn't even reach them...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to do something over and over again?\n\nPatient: Over and over? Nah, I don't really have anything like that. I just do things once and move on, usually. But man, talking about doing things over and over... you know what I hear over and over? The backup alarm on the forklifts at work. Beep, beep, beep, all day long. It gets stuck in your head. \n\nThere's this one guy, Gary, who drives the forklift like he's in a racecar or something. He's always slamming on the brakes and dropping pallets too hard. He dropped a whole pallet of dog food last month, and those bags just exploded everywhere. It looked like a gravel pit. Dog food smells terrible when there's that much of it just sitting out. It kind of reminds me of this time I went fishing with my uncle when I was a kid, and he had this bucket of bait that he left in the sun for like two days. It was just awful. We were trying to catch catfish, but all we caught were these tiny little sunfish. Sunfish are so annoying because they just steal your bait and run. My uncle had this crazy expensive fishing rod, it was made of carbon fiber or something. Carbon fiber is super strong, they use it in race cars and airplanes. I always thought it would be cool to fly an airplane, but I'm kind of scared of heights. Not like, terrified, but I get that weird dropping feeling in my stomach if I look down from a tall building. Like the Willis Tower in Chicago, they have those glass boxes you can stand in that stick out from the building. I saw a video of a guy proposing to his girlfriend in one of those, and he dropped the ring! Can you imagine? Just watching a diamond ring fall like a hundred stories... I wonder where it ended up. Probably hit some taxi down below. Taxis are so yellow...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Specific way? No, I don't really have any weird routines like that. I just do things however they need getting done. But speaking of doing things a specific way, my mom is crazy about how the dishwasher gets loaded. Like, she has this whole system where the forks have to face up, but the knives have to face down, and the plates have to go in by size. 

If you put a bowl in the wrong spot, she'll literally take the whole thing apart and repack it. It takes her like twenty minutes just to load it. She says it's about water flow or something. Water flow is crazy, though. Did you know that the Colorado River carved out the entire Grand Canyon just by flowing over the rock for millions of years? That's insane power. I went to the Grand Canyon when I was in high school. We drove the whole way in this beat-up Honda Civic that didn't have working AC. It was the middle of July, so we had to roll the windows down, and the wind was just blasting us the whole time. My hair was a complete disaster by the time we got there. I actually tried to cut my own hair once during lockdown, just with some kitchen scissors. It looked so bad, like I had a fight with a lawnmower. Lawnmowers always smell like fresh cut grass and gasoline. That gasoline smell is weirdly good, you know? Like how some people like the smell of Sharpies. I had a teacher in middle school, Mr. Henderson, who used to sniff the dry erase markers before he wrote on the board. He taught geography. He had all these maps pulled down over the chalkboard, and he'd point at countries with this long wooden stick. I always wondered where he got that stick, it looked like a pool cue that got sanded down...

I tried to get a ginger ale on Tuesday, and the machine just swallowed my money and started making this loud humming noise. \n\nIt sounded exactly like the lawnmower my dad used to have. It was this old red Honda mower, and you had to pull the cord like ten times just to get the engine to catch. The grass in our backyard was always way too tall because we'd put off mowing it until the city threatened to fine us. My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, he'd always glare at us over the chain-link fence when we finally did it. He grew these massive heirloom tomatoes on the other side. One time a squirrel took a single bite out of his biggest tomato and left it sitting right on the fence post...\n\nDoctor: Do you think these things you're doing at the warehouse are causing problems for you in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose it is causing some problems. Like, I’ve lost my forklift duties at the warehouse. People keep telling me they can’t count on me or keep things safe around me anymore. It’s a real pain. And I’ve fallen way behind in my community-college classes, too. I’m taking this astronomy class, and the professor always wears these bright yellow bowties. It looks exactly like the color of a school bus. I used to ride bus number 42 when I was in middle school, and the driver, Mrs. Gable, she always chewed that really loud bubblegum. The pink kind that smells like medicine? \n\nAnyway, I also stopped showing up for my shifts at the church food-bank because of the whole... people saying they can't count on me thing. Which is weird because all we do at the food bank is stack cans of soup. Mostly chicken noodle. I had chicken noodle soup when I got the flu last year, and I had to stay in bed for four days watching old game shows. The Price is Right is the best one, especially when they play the Plinko game. I always wondered how heavy those little chips actually are, you know? They bounce around so much before they hit the bottom...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to achieve something specific or follow a certain rule when you're moving the boxes in those strange patterns?\n\nPatient: A rule? Um... no, I don't think so. I'm not really trying to achieve anything, and there's no rulebook I'm following or anything like that. It's just... I just do it. But speaking of rulebooks, my roommate bought this massive board game last weekend, and the manual for it was like forty pages long. It was ridiculous. \n\nWe tried playing it on the living room floor, but the carpet in our apartment is really shaggy. It's that weird, bright orange shag carpet from the seventies? My landlord absolutely refuses to replace it. He's this grumpy guy who drives a huge white pickup truck, and he always takes up two parking spaces right in front of the building. One time he left a half-eaten sandwich on the dashboard, and it just baked in the sun for like three days straight. It was turkey and swiss, I'm pretty sure. I can't stand swiss cheese, honestly. It has all those holes in it, which just seems like you're getting ripped off, you know? Like you're paying for air. I buy the block cheddar from the deli down the street instead. The lady who works there has this giant tattoo of a compass on her forearm...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these things you're doing are causing you a lot of stress or anxiety in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Stress? Anxiety? Um, no, I don't... I don't really feel stressed about the boxes. It's not like my heart is racing or anything. It's just what I do. But you know what actually does stress me out? The bus schedule in this city. It's completely unpredictable. I was waiting at the stop on 4th Street yesterday, and the sign said the bus would be there in ten minutes, but I ended up standing there for almost an hour. \n\nIt started drizzling, too, and I didn't have an umbrella. I used to have this really good umbrella, it was golf-sized and had these green and white stripes. I left it on a park bench last summer when I was feeding the ducks. There's this one duck at the pond, he has a weird little tuft of feathers on his head that makes him look like he's wearing a tiny hat. People always throw bread at him, but you're really supposed to give them peas or corn. Bread is actually terrible for their stomachs. I read that in a magazine at the dentist's office once. It was an old issue of National Geographic, the one with the penguins on the cover. Penguins are fascinating, the way they huddle together for warmth...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming urge to repeat certain actions or behaviors outside of the warehouse, like the way you arrange things or copy sounds?\n\nPatient: Urges? Like, outside of work? Um... no, not really. I don't feel like I have to do those things when I'm not at the warehouse. It's not like an urge or anything. But speaking of urges, I had this massive craving for a chili dog the other night. Like, out of nowhere. \n\nI ended up walking all the way down to that late-night diner on 5th Avenue, the one with the flickering neon sign. The 'E' in 'Diner' is completely burned out, so it just says 'Din r.' It's been like that for at least two years. Anyway, the guy working the grill there, he's got this huge bushy beard, and he always wears a baseball cap backwards. It’s a Dodgers cap, which is weird because we’re thousands of miles away from Los Angeles. I’ve never been to California, but I’ve heard the traffic there is an absolute nightmare. My cousin Greg moved out there to try and become a screenwriter, but he ended up just working at a car wash. He said he washed a celebrity's car once, but he couldn't remember who it was. It was a silver Porsche, though. Porsches are nice, but they seem really impractical if you need to buy groceries or move furniture...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or unhappy when you're not able to move the boxes in those specific patterns?\n\nPatient: Upset? Um, no. If I can't move them like that, I just... I don't. It doesn't make me unhappy or anything. But you know what actually did make me really upset? The vending machine in the break room at the warehouse. It totally ate my dollar on Tuesday. I just wanted a bag of those spicy jalapeño chips, but the metal coil stopped turning right when the bag was at the very edge. It just hung there. \n\nI tried shaking the machine, but it's like, bolted to the wall or something. My buddy Dave tried to help me reach up inside the flap to grab it, but his arm got stuck for a second. Dave's a pretty big guy, he used to play defensive tackle in high school. He still wears his varsity jacket everywhere, even though the leather on the sleeves is all cracked and it's missing a button. Buttons are so annoying when they fall off, I never know how to sew them back on. My grandma tried to teach me once, she had this old blue metal tin that used to have Danish butter cookies in it, but it was just full of needles and thread. It's such a massive disappointment when you open one of those tins expecting a cookie and it's just a bunch of sewing stuff...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or unhappy when you're not able to copy the beeping sounds or hand signals from the forklifts?\n\nPatient: Upset about the beeping? No, not really. If I can't make the noises or do the signals, it's fine. I don't get mad about it or anything. But speaking of beeping, the microwave in my apartment is driving me completely insane. It has this super high-pitched beep when the timer finishes, and it just keeps going every minute until you open the door. \n\nI was just trying to heat up a Hot Pocket a few days ago—the pepperoni kind, which is obviously the best, even though the crust always gets rock hard on the ends. I got a giant box of them at that wholesale club out on Route 9. You have to buy literally everything in bulk there. I saw this guy with a flatbed cart buying like, fifty giant bottles of ketchup. I have no idea who needs that much ketchup unless they run a hot dog stand or something. My uncle actually used to own a hot dog stand near the boardwalk, but he had to sell it because the city raised the permit fees. The city council is always raising taxes on stuff. They spent all this money on a new modern art statue for the park, but it just looks like a giant rusty metal pretzel. Pigeons sit on it all day, and pigeons are actually really smart birds, they can recognize human faces. I saw a video about it when I was taking a biology class at the community college. We had to use these really old microscopes that barely focused...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed when you cannot copy the beeping sounds or move the boxes in the specific patterns you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Distressed? No, I don't... I don't feel distressed. If I can't do the patterns or the sounds, it's just whatever. It doesn't bother me. But man, talking about distress, you know what really stresses me out? Trying to find a parking spot near my apartment building after 6 PM. It's impossible. \n\nThe street sweepers come on Thursdays, so everyone has to move their cars to the other side of the street, and it turns into this huge game of musical chairs. Last week, I had to park like four blocks away near this old laundromat. The laundromat has these massive industrial dryers that smell like burnt lint and cheap fabric softener. I used to go there before I got my own washer, but this lady would always hog like three folding tables at once. She had these tiny little yappy dogs she’d bring in, even though there was a huge 'No Pets' sign on the door. People just ignore signs all the time. Like at the warehouse, there's a sign that says 'Maximum Capacity 15 People' in the break room, but I swear there were like twenty of us in there during the holiday rush. The holidays are always so crazy with shipping, everyone ordering last-minute gifts online. I ordered my mom a scarf last year, but it got lost in the mail for three weeks. The tracking info just said 'In Transit' forever. I hate when it just says 'In Transit' and doesn't tell you where it actually is...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must perform certain rituals or behaviors in a specific way, even when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Like... doing things a certain way outside of work? Um, no, not really. I don't have anything like that when I'm just at home or out and about. But speaking of doing things a certain way, my neighbor across the hall is completely obsessed with his front door mat. He has to have it lined up perfectly with the edges of the doorframe. \n\nI saw him out there the other day with a tape measure, I'm not even kidding. A yellow Stanley tape measure. My dad used to have one of those, he kept it in this massive red toolbox in the garage. That toolbox weighed a ton because he never threw away any rusty screws or bent nails. He always said, 'You never know when you'll need a good nail.' But they weren't good, they were all crooked! We tried to build a birdhouse once when I was like, ten. We used those crooked nails and the roof ended up completely lopsided. A squirrel ended up moving into it instead of a bird. Squirrels are actually crazy agile, I watched one jump from a telephone wire to a tree branch that was like ten feet away. It just launched itself into the air like a tiny acrobat. I went to a circus once with acrobats, but the tent smelled super weird, kind of like old popcorn and damp hay...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you must perform certain actions or behaviors, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Actions I don't want to do? No, I don't think so. I just do what I need to do, you know? I don't feel forced to do anything weird. But talking about doing things you don't want to do, I had to help my buddy Dave move last weekend, and let me tell you, I really did not want to do that. He lives on the fourth floor of this walk-up building, and the stairs are super narrow. \n\nWe had to pivot this giant, ugly plaid couch around like three different corners. It was like that scene in Friends, you know, where they're yelling 'Pivot! Pivot!' I always thought that show was kind of overrated, to be honest. Like, how did they afford those huge apartments in New York City? New York is so expensive. I went there once for a school trip in middle school, and I bought a hot dog from a street cart that cost like eight dollars. Eight dollars! And the bun was kind of soggy. It had that weird yellow mustard on it, the kind that stains your shirt forever if you drop it. I ruined my favorite band t-shirt that way. It was a Foo Fighters shirt, the one with the alien on it. Aliens are a whole other thing, man. Have you seen those declassified Navy videos? The ones with the little tic-tac shaped things flying around? They move way too fast to be our jets. My cousin thinks they're from underwater bases, not space. He spends all day on these weird internet forums reading about deep-sea trenches. The ocean is crazy deep, like, there are fish down there that glow in the dark because sunlight doesn't even reach them...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to do something over and over again?\n\nPatient: Over and over? Nah, I don't really have anything like that. I just do things once and move on, usually. But man, talking about doing things over and over... you know what I hear over and over? The backup alarm on the forklifts at work. Beep, beep, beep, all day long. It gets stuck in your head. \n\nThere's this one guy, Gary, who drives the forklift like he's in a racecar or something. He's always slamming on the brakes and dropping pallets too hard. He dropped a whole pallet of dog food last month, and those bags just exploded everywhere. It looked like a gravel pit. Dog food smells terrible when there's that much of it just sitting out. It kind of reminds me of this time I went fishing with my uncle when I was a kid, and he had this bucket of bait that he left in the sun for like two days. It was just awful. We were trying to catch catfish, but all we caught were these tiny little sunfish. Sunfish are so annoying because they just steal your bait and run. My uncle had this crazy expensive fishing rod, it was made of carbon fiber or something. Carbon fiber is super strong, they use it in race cars and airplanes. I always thought it would be cool to fly an airplane, but I'm kind of scared of heights. Not like, terrified, but I get that weird dropping feeling in my stomach if I look down from a tall building. Like the Willis Tower in Chicago, they have those glass boxes you can stand in that stick out from the building. I saw a video of a guy proposing to his girlfriend in one of those, and he dropped the ring! Can you imagine? Just watching a diamond ring fall like a hundred stories... I wonder where it ended up. Probably hit some taxi down below. Taxis are so yellow...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to do something in a very specific way, even if it doesn't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: Specific way? No, I don't really have any weird routines like that. I just do things however they need getting done. But speaking of doing things a specific way, my mom is crazy about how the dishwasher gets loaded. Like, she has this whole system where the forks have to face up, but the knives have to face down, and the plates have to go in by size. \n\nIf you put a bowl in the wrong spot, she'll literally take the whole thing apart and repack it. It takes her like twenty minutes just to load it. She says it's about water flow or something. Water flow is crazy, though. Did you know that the Colorado River carved out the entire Grand Canyon just by flowing over the rock for millions of years? That's insane power. I went to the Grand Canyon when I was in high school. We drove the whole way in this beat-up Honda Civic that didn't have working AC. It was the middle of July, so we had to roll the windows down, and the wind was just blasting us the whole time. My hair was a complete disaster by the time we got there. I actually tried to cut my own hair once during lockdown, just with some kitchen scissors. It looked so bad, like I had a fight with a lawnmower. Lawnmowers always smell like fresh cut grass and gasoline. That gasoline smell is weirdly good, you know? Like how some people like the smell of Sharpies. I had a teacher in middle school, Mr. Henderson, who used to sniff the dry erase markers before he wrote on the board. He taught geography. He had all these maps pulled down over the chalkboard, and he'd point at countries with this long wooden stick. I always wondered where he got that stick, it looked like a pool cue that got sanded down...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. No, I don't really do anything like that. I don't have to repeat stuff to feel okay or stop bad things. Bad things just happen anyway, you know? Like, you can't prevent a flat tire by tapping a doorknob. 

Speaking of flat tires, I got one on my way to work last Tuesday on I-95. Just a total blowout, sounded like a gunshot. I had to pull over onto this tiny shoulder, and cars are just whipping past me at like 80 miles an hour. It shakes your whole car when a semi goes by. Those truck drivers, man, they work crazy hours. I talked to this one guy at a diner once who said he drove from Seattle to Miami in like three days. He was drinking this coffee that looked like motor oil. I can't drink coffee, it makes my hands shake too much. I usually just drink those energy drinks, the ones in the big green cans. But you gotta be careful with those, my buddy drank three of them before a shift and he threw up in the breakroom trash can. The janitor, this old guy named Earl, was so mad. Earl's been working there for like thirty years, he's got this huge ring of keys that jingles when he walks down the hall. It sounds like sleigh bells. I always loved Christmas when I was a kid, mostly for the food. My grandma used to make this sweet potato casserole with the marshmallows on top. The marshmallows would get all brown and crispy on the edges...

He still wears his varsity jacket everywhere, even though the leather on the sleeves is all cracked and it's missing a button. Like at the warehouse, there's a sign that says 'Maximum Capacity 15 People' in the break room, but I swear there were like twenty of us in there during the holiday rush. The tracking info just said 'In Transit' forever. But speaking of doing things a certain way, my neighbor across the hall is completely obsessed with his front door mat. He has to have it lined up perfectly with the edges of the doorframe. \n\nI saw him out there the other day with a tape measure, I'm not even kidding. A yellow Stanley tape measure. He lives on the fourth floor of this walk-up building, and the stairs are super narrow. \n\nWe had to pivot this giant, ugly plaid couch around like three different corners. The ocean is crazy deep, like, there are fish down there that glow in the dark because sunlight doesn't even reach them...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to do something over and over again?\n\nPatient: Over and over? Nah, I don't really have anything like that. I just do things once and move on, usually. But man, talking about doing things over and over... you know what I hear over and over? The backup alarm on the forklifts at work. Beep, beep, beep, all day long. It gets stuck in your head. \n\nThere's this one guy, Gary, who drives the forklift like he's in a racecar or something. Dog food smells terrible when there's that much of it just sitting out. We were trying to catch catfish, but all we caught were these tiny little sunfish. Sunfish are so annoying because they just steal your bait and run. My uncle had this crazy expensive fishing rod, it was made of carbon fiber or something. Carbon fiber is super strong, they use it in race cars and airplanes. I always thought it would be cool to fly an airplane, but I'm kind of scared of heights. Not like, terrified, but I get that weird dropping feeling in my stomach if I look down from a tall building. Like the Willis Tower in Chicago, they have those glass boxes you can stand in that stick out from the building. I saw a video of a guy proposing to his girlfriend in one of those, and he dropped the ring! Can you imagine? Just watching a diamond ring fall like a hundred stories... I wonder where it ended up. Probably hit some taxi down below. Taxis are so yellow...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to do something in a very specific way, even if it doesn't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: Specific way? My hair was a complete disaster by the time we got there. I actually tried to cut my own hair once during lockdown, just with some kitchen scissors. Rituals? Like outside of work? Nah, I don't do anything like that. I just go home, maybe watch some TV or something. I don't have any special routines. 

Although, speaking of TV, I started watching this documentary the other night about ancient Egypt. It was on one of those random channels you only find when you're flipping through at 2 AM. They were talking about how they built the pyramids, and man, the stones they used were massive. Like, tons and tons of rock. They said they might have used wet sand to slide them along, which is wild to think about. Sand is so weird, you know? It gets everywhere. I went to the beach last summer in Maryland, Ocean City, and I swear I was still finding sand in my shoes like three months later. The boardwalk there is pretty cool, though. They have these huge buckets of French fries. Thrasher's, I think it's called. You put vinegar on them instead of ketchup. Vinegar is such a strong smell. We used to use it to clean the coffee pot when I lived in the dorms for a bit. My roommate, Steve, he was a total neat freak. He'd scrub the sink every single day. He was studying accounting or something with numbers. Numbers always gave me a headache. That's why I dropped out, honestly. Just too much math. I'd rather be moving boxes around at the warehouse, at least you don't have to do algebra to drive a forklift... well, mostly. You just gotta make sure you don't tip it over.

My dad used to have one of those, he kept it in this massive red toolbox in the garage. That toolbox weighed a ton because he never threw away any rusty screws or bent nails. He always said, 'You never know when you'll need a good nail.' But they weren't good, they were all crooked! We tried to build a birdhouse once when I was like, ten. I went to a circus once with acrobats, but the tent smelled super weird, kind of like old popcorn and damp hay...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you must perform certain actions or behaviors, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Actions I don't want to do? No, I don't think so. I just do what I need to do, you know? I don't feel forced to do anything weird. But talking about doing things you don't want to do, I had to help my buddy Dave move last weekend, and let me tell you, I really did not want to do that. He lives on the fourth floor of this walk-up building, and the stairs are super narrow. \n\nWe had to pivot this giant, ugly plaid couch around like three different corners. It was like that scene in Friends, you know, where they're yelling 'Pivot! Pivot!' I always thought that show was kind of overrated, to be honest. Like, how did they afford those huge apartments in New York City? New York is so expensive. I went there once for a school trip in middle school, and I bought a hot dog from a street cart that cost like eight dollars. Eight dollars! And the bun was kind of soggy. It had that weird yellow mustard on it, the kind that stains your shirt forever if you drop it. I ruined my favorite band t-shirt that way. It was a Foo Fighters shirt, the one with the alien on it. Aliens are a whole other thing, man. Have you seen those declassified Navy videos? The ones with the little tic-tac shaped things flying around? They move way too fast to be our jets. My cousin thinks they're from underwater bases, not space. He spends all day on these weird internet forums reading about deep-sea trenches. The ocean is crazy deep, like, there are fish down there that glow in the dark because sunlight doesn't even reach them...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to do something over and over again?\n\nPatient: Over and over? Nah, I don't really have anything like that. I just do things once and move on, usually. But man, talking about doing things over and over... you know what I hear over and over? The backup alarm on the forklifts at work. Beep, beep, beep, all day long. It gets stuck in your head. \n\nThere's this one guy, Gary, who drives the forklift like he's in a racecar or something. He's always slamming on the brakes and dropping pallets too hard. He dropped a whole pallet of dog food last month, and those bags just exploded everywhere. It looked like a gravel pit. Dog food smells terrible when there's that much of it just sitting out. It kind of reminds me of this time I went fishing with my uncle when I was a kid, and he had this bucket of bait that he left in the sun for like two days. It was just awful. We were trying to catch catfish, but all we caught were these tiny little sunfish. Sunfish are so annoying because they just steal your bait and run. My uncle had this crazy expensive fishing rod, it was made of carbon fiber or something. Carbon fiber is super strong, they use it in race cars and airplanes. I always thought it would be cool to fly an airplane, but I'm kind of scared of heights. Not like, terrified, but I get that weird dropping feeling in my stomach if I look down from a tall building. Like the Willis Tower in Chicago, they have those glass boxes you can stand in that stick out from the building. I saw a video of a guy proposing to his girlfriend in one of those, and he dropped the ring! Can you imagine? Just watching a diamond ring fall like a hundred stories... I wonder where it ended up. Probably hit some taxi down below. Taxis are so yellow...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to do something in a very specific way, even if it doesn't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: Specific way? No, I don't really have any weird routines like that. I just do things however they need getting done. But speaking of doing things a specific way, my mom is crazy about how the dishwasher gets loaded. Like, she has this whole system where the forks have to face up, but the knives have to face down, and the plates have to go in by size. \n\nIf you put a bowl in the wrong spot, she'll literally take the whole thing apart and repack it. It takes her like twenty minutes just to load it. She says it's about water flow or something. Water flow is crazy, though. Did you know that the Colorado River carved out the entire Grand Canyon just by flowing over the rock for millions of years? That's insane power. I went to the Grand Canyon when I was in high school. We drove the whole way in this beat-up Honda Civic that didn't have working AC. It was the middle of July, so we had to roll the windows down, and the wind was just blasting us the whole time. My hair was a complete disaster by the time we got there. I actually tried to cut my own hair once during lockdown, just with some kitchen scissors. It looked so bad, like I had a fight with a lawnmower. Lawnmowers always smell like fresh cut grass and gasoline. That gasoline smell is weirdly good, you know? Like how some people like the smell of Sharpies. I had a teacher in middle school, Mr. Henderson, who used to sniff the dry erase markers before he wrote on the board. He taught geography. He had all these maps pulled down over the chalkboard, and he'd point at countries with this long wooden stick. I always wondered where he got that stick, it looked like a pool cue that got sanded down...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat certain actions or behaviors to feel right or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do anything like that. I don't have to repeat stuff to feel okay or stop bad things. Bad things just happen anyway, you know? Like, you can't prevent a flat tire by tapping a doorknob. \n\nSpeaking of flat tires, I got one on my way to work last Tuesday on I-95. Just a total blowout, sounded like a gunshot. I had to pull over onto this tiny shoulder, and cars are just whipping past me at like 80 miles an hour. It shakes your whole car when a semi goes by. Those truck drivers, man, they work crazy hours. I talked to this one guy at a diner once who said he drove from Seattle to Miami in like three days. He was drinking this coffee that looked like motor oil. I can't drink coffee, it makes my hands shake too much. I usually just drink those energy drinks, the ones in the big green cans. But you gotta be careful with those, my buddy drank three of them before a shift and he threw up in the breakroom trash can. The janitor, this old guy named Earl, was so mad. Earl's been working there for like thirty years, he's got this huge ring of keys that jingles when he walks down the hall. It sounds like sleigh bells. I always loved Christmas when I was a kid, mostly for the food. My grandma used to make this sweet potato casserole with the marshmallows on top. The marshmallows would get all brown and crispy on the edges...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific rituals or behaviors when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Like outside of work? Nah, I don't do anything like that. I just go home, maybe watch some TV or something. I don't have any special routines. \n\nAlthough, speaking of TV, I started watching this documentary the other night about ancient Egypt. It was on one of those random channels you only find when you're flipping through at 2 AM. They were talking about how they built the pyramids, and man, the stones they used were massive. Like, tons and tons of rock. They said they might have used wet sand to slide them along, which is wild to think about. Sand is so weird, you know? It gets everywhere. I went to the beach last summer in Maryland, Ocean City, and I swear I was still finding sand in my shoes like three months later. The boardwalk there is pretty cool, though. They have these huge buckets of French fries. Thrasher's, I think it's called. You put vinegar on them instead of ketchup. Vinegar is such a strong smell. We used to use it to clean the coffee pot when I lived in the dorms for a bit. My roommate, Steve, he was a total neat freak. He'd scrub the sink every single day. He was studying accounting or something with numbers. Numbers always gave me a headache. That's why I dropped out, honestly. Just too much math. I'd rather be moving boxes around at the warehouse, at least you don't have to do algebra to drive a forklift... well, mostly. You just gotta make sure you don't tip it over.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Well, upset? I don't know if upset is the right word. But, yeah, I guess that's why they brought me in here. At the warehouse, I'll be moving the boxes, and I just start doing them in these strange little patterns, you know? And everyone starts yelling at me to stop, but I just... I also do this thing where I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, and I'll copy the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up. Beep, beep, beep. And I copy the hand signals the spotters use. 

Spotters are important, though. You don't want to back over somebody's foot. My uncle lost a toe once, not from a forklift, but from a lawnmower. Speaking of lawnmowers, I was talking about that smell earlier, right? Grass and gasoline. He had this old riding mower, a John Deere, painted that bright green. Green is a good color. It's the color of those energy drink cans I was talking about. I wonder who designs those cans. They probably make a lot of money just picking colors and fonts. I tried to learn graphic design once, downloaded some software, but it just kept crashing my laptop. It was this old Dell, weighed like ten pounds. It got so hot you could probably fry an egg on it. I like my eggs over easy, with some hot sauce. Texas Pete is the best kind. We used to put Texas Pete on everything when I was in the dorms with Steve. Steve couldn't handle spicy food, though. One time he ate a jalapeño popper and his face turned the color of a stop sign...

The bus schedule in this city. It's completely unpredictable. I was waiting at the stop on 4th Street yesterday, and the sign said the bus would be there in ten minutes, but I ended up standing there for almost an hour. \n\nIt started drizzling, too, and I didn't have an umbrella. I used to have this really good umbrella, it was golf-sized and had these green and white stripes. I left it on a park bench last summer when I was feeding the ducks. People always throw bread at him, but you're really supposed to give them peas or corn. Bread is actually terrible for their stomachs. I read that in a magazine at the dentist's office once. Anyway, the guy working the grill there, he's got this huge bushy beard, and he always wears a baseball cap backwards. He still wears his varsity jacket everywhere, even though the leather on the sleeves is all cracked and it's missing a button. Buttons are so annoying when they fall off, I never know how to sew them back on. My grandma tried to teach me once, she had this old blue metal tin that used to have Danish butter cookies in it, but it was just full of needles and thread. It's such a massive disappointment when you open one of those tins expecting a cookie and it's just a bunch of sewing stuff...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or unhappy when you're not able to copy the beeping sounds or hand signals from the forklifts?\n\nPatient: Upset about the beeping? No, not really. If I can't make the noises or do the signals, it's fine. I don't get mad about it or anything. But speaking of beeping, the microwave in my apartment is driving me completely insane. It has this super high-pitched beep when the timer finishes, and it just keeps going every minute until you open the door. \n\nI was just trying to heat up a Hot Pocket a few days ago—the pepperoni kind, which is obviously the best, even though the crust always gets rock hard on the ends. I got a giant box of them at that wholesale club out on Route 9. You have to buy literally everything in bulk there. I saw this guy with a flatbed cart buying like, fifty giant bottles of ketchup. My uncle actually used to own a hot dog stand near the boardwalk, but he had to sell it because the city raised the permit fees. The city council is always raising taxes on stuff. They spent all this money on a new modern art statue for the park, but it just looks like a giant rusty metal pretzel. Last week, I had to park like four blocks away near this old laundromat. The laundromat has these massive industrial dryers that smell like burnt lint and cheap fabric softener. I used to go there before I got my own washer, but this lady would always hog like three folding tables at once. She had these tiny little yappy dogs she’d bring in, even though there was a huge 'No Pets' sign on the door. People just ignore signs all the time. Like at the warehouse, there's a sign that says 'Maximum Capacity 15 People' in the break room, but I swear there were like twenty of us in there during the holiday rush. The holidays are always so crazy with shipping, everyone ordering last-minute gifts online. I ordered my mom a scarf last year, but it got lost in the mail for three weeks. The tracking info just said 'In Transit' forever. I hate when it just says 'In Transit' and doesn't tell you where it actually is...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must perform certain rituals or behaviors in a specific way, even when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Like... doing things a certain way outside of work? Um, no, not really. I don't have anything like that when I'm just at home or out and about. But speaking of doing things a certain way, my neighbor across the hall is completely obsessed with his front door mat. He has to have it lined up perfectly with the edges of the doorframe. \n\nI saw him out there the other day with a tape measure, I'm not even kidding. A yellow Stanley tape measure. My dad used to have one of those, he kept it in this massive red toolbox in the garage. That toolbox weighed a ton because he never threw away any rusty screws or bent nails. He always said, 'You never know when you'll need a good nail.' But they weren't good, they were all crooked! We tried to build a birdhouse once when I was like, ten. We used those crooked nails and the roof ended up completely lopsided. A squirrel ended up moving into it instead of a bird. Squirrels are actually crazy agile, I watched one jump from a telephone wire to a tree branch that was like ten feet away. It just launched itself into the air like a tiny acrobat. I went to a circus once with acrobats, but the tent smelled super weird, kind of like old popcorn and damp hay...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you must perform certain actions or behaviors, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Actions I don't want to do? No, I don't think so. I just do what I need to do, you know? I don't feel forced to do anything weird. But talking about doing things you don't want to do, I had to help my buddy Dave move last weekend, and let me tell you, I really did not want to do that. He lives on the fourth floor of this walk-up building, and the stairs are super narrow. \n\nWe had to pivot this giant, ugly plaid couch around like three different corners. It was like that scene in Friends, you know, where they're yelling 'Pivot! Pivot!' I always thought that show was kind of overrated, to be honest. Like, how did they afford those huge apartments in New York City? New York is so expensive. I went there once for a school trip in middle school, and I bought a hot dog from a street cart that cost like eight dollars. Eight dollars! And the bun was kind of soggy. It had that weird yellow mustard on it, the kind that stains your shirt forever if you drop it. I ruined my favorite band t-shirt that way. It was a Foo Fighters shirt, the one with the alien on it. Aliens are a whole other thing, man. Have you seen those declassified Navy videos? The ones with the little tic-tac shaped things flying around? They move way too fast to be our jets. My cousin thinks they're from underwater bases, not space. He spends all day on these weird internet forums reading about deep-sea trenches. The ocean is crazy deep, like, there are fish down there that glow in the dark because sunlight doesn't even reach them...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to do something over and over again?\n\nPatient: Over and over? Nah, I don't really have anything like that. I just do things once and move on, usually. But man, talking about doing things over and over... you know what I hear over and over? The backup alarm on the forklifts at work. Beep, beep, beep, all day long. It gets stuck in your head. \n\nThere's this one guy, Gary, who drives the forklift like he's in a racecar or something. He's always slamming on the brakes and dropping pallets too hard. He dropped a whole pallet of dog food last month, and those bags just exploded everywhere. It looked like a gravel pit. Dog food smells terrible when there's that much of it just sitting out. It kind of reminds me of this time I went fishing with my uncle when I was a kid, and he had this bucket of bait that he left in the sun for like two days. It was just awful. We were trying to catch catfish, but all we caught were these tiny little sunfish. Sunfish are so annoying because they just steal your bait and run. My uncle had this crazy expensive fishing rod, it was made of carbon fiber or something. Carbon fiber is super strong, they use it in race cars and airplanes. I always thought it would be cool to fly an airplane, but I'm kind of scared of heights. Not like, terrified, but I get that weird dropping feeling in my stomach if I look down from a tall building. Like the Willis Tower in Chicago, they have those glass boxes you can stand in that stick out from the building. I saw a video of a guy proposing to his girlfriend in one of those, and he dropped the ring! Can you imagine? Just watching a diamond ring fall like a hundred stories... I wonder where it ended up. Probably hit some taxi down below. Taxis are so yellow...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to do something in a very specific way, even if it doesn't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: Specific way? No, I don't really have any weird routines like that. I just do things however they need getting done. But speaking of doing things a specific way, my mom is crazy about how the dishwasher gets loaded. Like, she has this whole system where the forks have to face up, but the knives have to face down, and the plates have to go in by size. \n\nIf you put a bowl in the wrong spot, she'll literally take the whole thing apart and repack it. It takes her like twenty minutes just to load it. She says it's about water flow or something. Water flow is crazy, though. Did you know that the Colorado River carved out the entire Grand Canyon just by flowing over the rock for millions of years? That's insane power. I went to the Grand Canyon when I was in high school. We drove the whole way in this beat-up Honda Civic that didn't have working AC. It was the middle of July, so we had to roll the windows down, and the wind was just blasting us the whole time. My hair was a complete disaster by the time we got there. I actually tried to cut my own hair once during lockdown, just with some kitchen scissors. It looked so bad, like I had a fight with a lawnmower. Lawnmowers always smell like fresh cut grass and gasoline. That gasoline smell is weirdly good, you know? Like how some people like the smell of Sharpies. I had a teacher in middle school, Mr. Henderson, who used to sniff the dry erase markers before he wrote on the board. He taught geography. He had all these maps pulled down over the chalkboard, and he'd point at countries with this long wooden stick. I always wondered where he got that stick, it looked like a pool cue that got sanded down...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat certain actions or behaviors to feel right or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do anything like that. I don't have to repeat stuff to feel okay or stop bad things. Bad things just happen anyway, you know? Like, you can't prevent a flat tire by tapping a doorknob. \n\nSpeaking of flat tires, I got one on my way to work last Tuesday on I-95. Just a total blowout, sounded like a gunshot. I had to pull over onto this tiny shoulder, and cars are just whipping past me at like 80 miles an hour. It shakes your whole car when a semi goes by. Those truck drivers, man, they work crazy hours. I talked to this one guy at a diner once who said he drove from Seattle to Miami in like three days. He was drinking this coffee that looked like motor oil. I can't drink coffee, it makes my hands shake too much. I usually just drink those energy drinks, the ones in the big green cans. But you gotta be careful with those, my buddy drank three of them before a shift and he threw up in the breakroom trash can. The janitor, this old guy named Earl, was so mad. Earl's been working there for like thirty years, he's got this huge ring of keys that jingles when he walks down the hall. It sounds like sleigh bells. I always loved Christmas when I was a kid, mostly for the food. My grandma used to make this sweet potato casserole with the marshmallows on top. The marshmallows would get all brown and crispy on the edges...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific rituals or behaviors when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Like outside of work? Nah, I don't do anything like that. I just go home, maybe watch some TV or something. I don't have any special routines. \n\nAlthough, speaking of TV, I started watching this documentary the other night about ancient Egypt. It was on one of those random channels you only find when you're flipping through at 2 AM. They were talking about how they built the pyramids, and man, the stones they used were massive. Like, tons and tons of rock. They said they might have used wet sand to slide them along, which is wild to think about. Sand is so weird, you know? It gets everywhere. I went to the beach last summer in Maryland, Ocean City, and I swear I was still finding sand in my shoes like three months later. The boardwalk there is pretty cool, though. They have these huge buckets of French fries. Thrasher's, I think it's called. You put vinegar on them instead of ketchup. Vinegar is such a strong smell. We used to use it to clean the coffee pot when I lived in the dorms for a bit. My roommate, Steve, he was a total neat freak. He'd scrub the sink every single day. He was studying accounting or something with numbers. Numbers always gave me a headache. That's why I dropped out, honestly. Just too much math. I'd rather be moving boxes around at the warehouse, at least you don't have to do algebra to drive a forklift... well, mostly. You just gotta make sure you don't tip it over.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset when you're not allowed to move the boxes in your preferred pattern?\n\nPatient: Well, upset? I don't know if upset is the right word. But, yeah, I guess that's why they brought me in here. At the warehouse, I'll be moving the boxes, and I just start doing them in these strange little patterns, you know? And everyone starts yelling at me to stop, but I just... I also do this thing where I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, and I'll copy the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up. Beep, beep, beep. And I copy the hand signals the spotters use. \n\nSpotters are important, though. You don't want to back over somebody's foot. My uncle lost a toe once, not from a forklift, but from a lawnmower. Speaking of lawnmowers, I was talking about that smell earlier, right? Grass and gasoline. He had this old riding mower, a John Deere, painted that bright green. Green is a good color. It's the color of those energy drink cans I was talking about. I wonder who designs those cans. They probably make a lot of money just picking colors and fonts. I tried to learn graphic design once, downloaded some software, but it just kept crashing my laptop. It was this old Dell, weighed like ten pounds. It got so hot you could probably fry an egg on it. I like my eggs over easy, with some hot sauce. Texas Pete is the best kind. We used to put Texas Pete on everything when I was in the dorms with Steve. Steve couldn't handle spicy food, though. One time he ate a jalapeño popper and his face turned the color of a stop sign...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Anxious? I mean, I wouldn't say I feel anxious about it, really. It's just... I don't know, it's just what I do. I don't get all worked up if I can't do the patterns or the sounds, it's not like a panic attack or anything. Speaking of panic attacks, my sister used to get those all the time when she was in high school. She'd be getting ready for a test and just start hyperventilating. We had to get her these paper bags to breathe into. It's funny how a brown paper bag is supposed to fix everything, right? Like, you get a sandwich at the deli, it comes in a paper bag. You buy a bottle of something at the liquor store, paper bag. I used to save all those bags when I was a kid and make puppets out of them. Draw a little face with a Sharpie, maybe glue some yarn on for hair. Yarn is weird stuff, it's just string but fuzzy. My grandma had this whole basket of it, all tangled up in different colors. She was always knitting these itchy sweaters that I hated wearing. They'd make my neck break out in a rash. I prefer hoodies, honestly. Just a plain gray hoodie, you know? It's comfortable, you can put the hood up if it starts raining. Raining always messes up the traffic on I-95, like I was saying before. One time a squirrel took a single bite out of his biggest tomato and left it sitting right on the fence post...\n\nDoctor: Do you think these things you're doing at the warehouse are causing problems for you in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose it is causing some problems. Like, I’ve lost my forklift duties at the warehouse. People keep telling me they can’t count on me or keep things safe around me anymore. It’s a real pain. And I’ve fallen way behind in my community-college classes, too. I’m taking this astronomy class, and the professor always wears these bright yellow bowties. It looks exactly like the color of a school bus. I used to ride bus number 42 when I was in middle school, and the driver, Mrs. Gable, she always chewed that really loud bubblegum. Which is weird because all we do at the food bank is stack cans of soup. Mostly chicken noodle. I had chicken noodle soup when I got the flu last year, and I had to stay in bed for four days watching old game shows. The Price is Right is the best one, especially when they play the Plinko game. I always wondered how heavy those little chips actually are, you know? They bounce around so much before they hit the bottom...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to achieve something specific or follow a certain rule when you're moving the boxes in those strange patterns?\n\nPatient: A rule? But speaking of rulebooks, my roommate bought this massive board game last weekend, and the manual for it was like forty pages long. It was ridiculous. \n\nWe tried playing it on the living room floor, but the carpet in our apartment is really shaggy. It's that weird, bright orange shag carpet from the seventies? One time he left a half-eaten sandwich on the dashboard, and it just baked in the sun for like three days straight. It was turkey and swiss, I'm pretty sure. I can't stand swiss cheese, honestly.
