I don't get all worked up if I can't do the patterns or the sounds, it's not like a panic attack or anything. Speaking of panic attacks, my sister used to get those all the time when she was in high school. She'd be getting ready for a test and just start hyperventilating. We had to get her these paper bags to breathe into. It's funny how a brown paper bag is supposed to fix everything, right? Like, you get a sandwich at the deli, it comes in a paper bag. You buy a bottle of something at the liquor store, paper bag. I used to save all those bags when I was a kid and make puppets out of them. Draw a little face with a Sharpie, maybe glue some yarn on for hair. Yarn is weird stuff, it's just string but fuzzy. My grandma had this whole basket of it, all tangled up in different colors. She was always knitting these itchy sweaters that I hated wearing. They'd make my neck break out in a rash. I prefer hoodies, honestly. Just a plain gray hoodie, you know? It's comfortable, you can put the hood up if it starts raining. Raining always messes up the traffic on I-95, like I was saying before. People forget how to drive the second a drop of water hits the windshield...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset when you're not allowed to copy the beeping sounds of the forklifts?\n\nPatient: Upset? But I don't get angry if someone tells me to knock it off. \n\nKnocking things off reminds me of this cat my neighbor used to have. This big orange tabby named Garfield, real original, right? He used to sit on the fence between our yards and just bat at whatever was up there until it fell off. Pinecones, leaves, an old tennis ball once. Tennis balls have that weird fuzzy texture, kinda like a peach. I love peaches, especially in the summer when they're super ripe and the juice gets all over your hands. We used to go to this orchard up past Baltimore to pick them. You'd get these huge baskets, peck baskets they called them. A peck is a weird measurement. Like, a bushel and a peck. It sounds like something out of an old song. My mom used to listen to all those oldies stations in the car when she drove me to school. Motown, mostly. The Supremes, The Temptations. They all wore those matching suits with the sparkly lapels. Suits always look so uncomfortable, especially the ties. I wore a tie to my high school graduation and I felt like I was choking the whole time. It was this awful maroon color, too. Maroon is basically just brown trying to be red, if you think about it. I prefer blue, like the ocean... well, the ocean isn't really blue, it's more like a murky green when you actually get in it. At least at Ocean City it is. The water there is always freezing, even in August. You gotta run in real fast and just dunk under before you lose your nerve...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the patterns you make with the boxes or the way you copy the beeping sounds have to be symmetrical or perfectly ordered?\n\nPatient: Symmetrical? He'd stand at the chalkboard with this giant wooden compass, drawing these huge circles and talking about pi. Pi is 3.14159... I used to know like twenty digits of it because we had a contest in middle school. The kid who won got a whole pie, a cherry one. Cherry pie is okay, but I prefer apple. Especially when it's warm with a scoop of vanilla ice cream on top. A la mode, they call it. Fancy French word for just putting ice cream on something. You could walk for miles without ever going outside. Which is good, because it was snowing like crazy up there. Snow is nice for about an hour, and then it just turns into this gray, slushy mess on the side of the road. It ruins your shoes, too. I had these nice white sneakers that got totally wrecked...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. He was studying accounting or something with numbers. Numbers always gave me a headache. That's why I dropped out, honestly. Just too much math. I'd rather be moving boxes around at the warehouse, at least you don't have to do algebra to drive a forklift... well, mostly. You just gotta make sure you don't tip it over.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset when you're not allowed to move the boxes in your preferred pattern?\n\nPatient: Well, upset? I don't know if upset is the right word. But, yeah, I guess that's why they brought me in here. At the warehouse, I'll be moving the boxes, and I just start doing them in these strange little patterns, you know? And everyone starts yelling at me to stop, but I just... I also do this thing where I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, and I'll copy the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up. Beep, beep, beep. And I copy the hand signals the spotters use. \n\nSpotters are important, though. You don't want to back over somebody's foot. My uncle lost a toe once, not from a forklift, but from a lawnmower. Speaking of lawnmowers, I was talking about that smell earlier, right? Grass and gasoline. He had this old riding mower, a John Deere, painted that bright green. Green is a good color. It's the color of those energy drink cans I was talking about. I wonder who designs those cans. They probably make a lot of money just picking colors and fonts. I tried to learn graphic design once, downloaded some software, but it just kept crashing my laptop. It was this old Dell, weighed like ten pounds. It got so hot you could probably fry an egg on it. I like my eggs over easy, with some hot sauce. Texas Pete is the best kind. We used to put Texas Pete on everything when I was in the dorms with Steve. Steve couldn't handle spicy food, though. One time he ate a jalapeño popper and his face turned the color of a stop sign...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not allowed to move the boxes in your preferred pattern or copy the beeping sounds?\n\nPatient: Anxious? I mean, I wouldn't say I feel anxious about it, really. It's just... I don't know, it's just what I do. I don't get all worked up if I can't do the patterns or the sounds, it's not like a panic attack or anything. Speaking of panic attacks, my sister used to get those all the time when she was in high school. She'd be getting ready for a test and just start hyperventilating. We had to get her these paper bags to breathe into. It's funny how a brown paper bag is supposed to fix everything, right? Like, you get a sandwich at the deli, it comes in a paper bag. You buy a bottle of something at the liquor store, paper bag. I used to save all those bags when I was a kid and make puppets out of them. Draw a little face with a Sharpie, maybe glue some yarn on for hair. Yarn is weird stuff, it's just string but fuzzy. My grandma had this whole basket of it, all tangled up in different colors. She was always knitting these itchy sweaters that I hated wearing. They'd make my neck break out in a rash. I prefer hoodies, honestly. Just a plain gray hoodie, you know? It's comfortable, you can put the hood up if it starts raining. Raining always messes up the traffic on I-95, like I was saying before. People forget how to drive the second a drop of water hits the windshield...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset when you're not allowed to copy the beeping sounds of the forklifts?\n\nPatient: Upset? Nah, not really upset. I don't get mad or anything if they tell me to stop with the beeping. It's just a thing that happens, you know? Like I just do it. But I don't get angry if someone tells me to knock it off. \n\nKnocking things off reminds me of this cat my neighbor used to have. This big orange tabby named Garfield, real original, right? He used to sit on the fence between our yards and just bat at whatever was up there until it fell off. Pinecones, leaves, an old tennis ball once. Tennis balls have that weird fuzzy texture, kinda like a peach. I love peaches, especially in the summer when they're super ripe and the juice gets all over your hands. We used to go to this orchard up past Baltimore to pick them. You'd get these huge baskets, peck baskets they called them. A peck is a weird measurement. Like, a bushel and a peck. It sounds like something out of an old song. My mom used to listen to all those oldies stations in the car when she drove me to school. Motown, mostly. The Supremes, The Temptations. They all wore those matching suits with the sparkly lapels. Suits always look so uncomfortable, especially the ties. I wore a tie to my high school graduation and I felt like I was choking the whole time. It was this awful maroon color, too. Maroon is basically just brown trying to be red, if you think about it. I prefer blue, like the ocean... well, the ocean isn't really blue, it's more like a murky green when you actually get in it. At least at Ocean City it is. The water there is always freezing, even in August. You gotta run in real fast and just dunk under before you lose your nerve...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the patterns you make with the boxes or the way you copy the beeping sounds have to be symmetrical or perfectly ordered?\n\nPatient: Symmetrical? Oh, no, it's not like that. I don't really care if it's perfectly ordered or symmetrical or whatever. It's just... I just put them how I put them, and the sounds are just the sounds. There's no big rule about it having to be perfect. \n\nPerfect is a weird concept anyway, you know? Like, my high school geometry teacher, Mr. Harrison, he was obsessed with perfect circles. He'd stand at the chalkboard with this giant wooden compass, drawing these huge circles and talking about pi. Pi is 3.14159... I used to know like twenty digits of it because we had a contest in middle school. The kid who won got a whole pie, a cherry one. Cherry pie is okay, but I prefer apple. Especially when it's warm with a scoop of vanilla ice cream on top. A la mode, they call it. Fancy French word for just putting ice cream on something. I took French for two years in high school, but all I remember is how to ask to go to the bathroom. \"Puis-je aller aux toilettes?\" It's funny how that's the one thing they make sure you know. When I went to Montreal with my buddies for a weekend, I tried to use it, but everyone there just spoke English back to me. We went to this huge underground mall there, it was crazy. You could walk for miles without ever going outside. Which is good, because it was snowing like crazy up there. Snow is nice for about an hour, and then it just turns into this gray, slushy mess on the side of the road. It ruins your shoes, too. I had these nice white sneakers that got totally wrecked..."
  }
